4.11        Computational Constraints and Exploratory

            Research Boundaries

The research programme was conducted under practical constraints concerning
computational resources, dataset availability, independent sample support, and the time
required for repeated fitting and evaluation.
       These conditions influenced the selection of cached representations, compact
prediction heads, limited hyperparameter grids, and bounded architectural follow-up


                                            119
Chapter 4. Research Design and Shared Methodology


experiments.
         Frozen visual extraction reduces repeated encoder computation during grouped
prediction-head fitting. Compact statistical features provide a low-dimensional sensor
representation. Limited selection grids restrict the number of configurations examined
during model development.
         Such choices define the evaluated prediction procedures and their computational
scope.
         They do not establish that the selected architectures are computationally
optimal or that alternative configurations would necessarily perform worse.
         Similarly, a model evaluated under a restricted fitting budget cannot be declared
intrinsically unsuitable solely because its performance is below that of a conventional
reference.
         Longer fitting, alternative representations, different preprocessing, or additional
supervision may influence the result. Their relevance must be established through the
corresponding comparisons.
         Unfinished experimental configurations also remain distinct from completed
configurations that produce negative findings.
         An unfinished configuration provides no empirical estimate of the performance it
might have achieved.


4.11.1 Computational Measurement Boundaries

Computational feasibility is part of evaluating industrial monitoring architectures.
         However, measurements of computational performance must identify which
operations are included.
         Feature extraction time, cached-representation processing, prediction-head
fitting, and end-to-end inference describe different computational workloads.
         The relevant measurement boundary may also depend on hardware, batch size,
data loading, preprocessing, synchronisation, and the availability of previously
extracted representations.


                                             120
Chapter 4. Research Design and Shared Methodology


       A rapid forward-pass measurement does not independently establish real-time
monitoring capability because obtaining the required observation window can introduce
additional delay.
       Consequently, computational measurements are interpreted based on the stages
included in the recorded procedure.
       The available timings provide bounded evidence about the cost of the
corresponding components rather than establishing complete operational performance
in an industrial installation.


4.11.2 Exploratory Versus Confirmatory Interpretation

The research is predominantly exploratory and retrospective.
       The original scientific questions establish the purpose of the investigation, while
subsequent dataset qualification, architecture selection, and controlled follow-ups
respond to the observations and limitations identified during its development.
       Repeated examination of assessment populations, selecting follow-up
experiments after inspecting results, and later protocol refinements limit the
confirmatory interpretation of the resulting performance improvements.
       Although individual model fits can exclude their designated outer assessment
observations, this separation does not remove the influence of previous assessment
exposure on the broader research process.
       The completed experiments nevertheless provide useful comparative evidence
concerning the investigated mechanisms.
       Their scientific value arises from defining the evaluated prediction targets,
retaining credible reference models, documenting which conditions change between
comparisons, and identifying the boundaries of the supported conclusions.
       A future confirmatory investigation would require specifying the target
definition, observation horizon, cohort construction, model-selection procedure,
principal comparisons, corruption operators, evaluation metrics, and relevant decision
criteria before opening an independent assessment population.


                                            121
Chapter 4. Research Design and Shared Methodology


       Such an investigation would provide a stronger basis for establishing whether the
observed architectural benefits persist beyond the current exploratory development
history.


4.11.3 Methodological Synthesis

The methods in this chapter explain what each comparison can—and cannot—show.
       Target validity and modality correspondence determine whether a proposed
prediction task can be meaningfully investigated. Representation definitions and fitting
procedures determine which information is retained and which modelling conditions
influence predictive performance.
       Unimodal predictors and simple fusion references provide the basis for assessing
incremental predictive value.
       Known modality absence and degraded but available observations require
different reliability interventions and comparison mechanisms.
       Grouped assessment, probability evaluation, class-specific metrics, and
uncertainty estimation establish the appropriate interpretation of the recorded
predictions.
       Reproducibility records and computational measurements further define the
evidence supporting the evaluated configurations.
       Taken together, these procedures apply requirements R1–R5 to the studies and
provide a consistent basis for answering the research questions.
       The requirements also identify where the available datasets and completed
experiments cannot support the intended comparison.
       The empirical studies therefore provide bounded evidence concerning the
suitability, predictive effectiveness, and reliability of the evaluated architectures under
their respective conditions.
       The following chapters apply this framework to the individual industrial and
robotic monitoring tasks. Each case study establishes its scientific motivation, describes
the corresponding investigation, reports the supported experimental findings, and
identifies the limitations governing their interpretation.
                                             122
Chapter 5

PHM Case Study: Target
Construction and Condition
Prediction

Across twenty gear runs in three held-out experiments, combining three groups of sensor
features gave a mean absolute error of 0.8238 percentage points against a photographic
condition proxy. A predictor that returned the training mean gave an error of 0.8042.
Both errors measure agreement with an image-processing rule applied to post-run tooth
photographs. The records contain no independent measurement of the damaged area
against which to validate that rule.
       PHM provides a diagnostic case of target validity and limited predictive benefit.
It was not designed as a negative control with an independently known null effect. Even
a reduction in prediction error would need independent validation before it could be
interpreted as better damage measurement. The study therefore distinguishes changes
in the target from changes in the predictor. Image-only and supplementary experiments
had different targets or assessment populations. Under the oﬀicial challenge protocol,
periodic photographs were available during training, whereas deployment relied on
sensor measurements (Prognostics and Health Management Society, 2026).




                                          123
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


Figure 5.1
PHM Scalar Regression From Post-Run Photographs




Note. The extraction code adds latency to the 768 image features, producing a
769-dimensional input to regression.


5.1     Study Objective and Relationship to the

        Research Questions

For SQ1, the image and sensor experiments assessed whether frozen visual features and
temporal sensor representations could reproduce a specified target. For SQ2, the
principal fusion comparison combined context, root-mean-square (RMS) vibration and
condition indicators, all available at inference. It did not test joint image–sensor
inference. For SQ3, a predictor that already returns nearly constant values can appear
insensitive to corruption, so its clean-input error matters. For SQ4, the training-mean
comparison asks whether measured sensor inputs improved prediction over a rule that
ignores them at inference. It cannot establish whether the photographic target
measures damage. That question requires a physical reference independent of the rule.



5.2     Dataset Overview and Acquisition Structure

The retained population contains EXP-A runs 1–5, EXP-B runs 1–7 and EXP-F runs
1–8: twenty nominal run units across three experiments. A run can contain multiple



                                            124
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


tooth photographs, camera-sequence views, high-frequency vibration members,
low-frequency context measurements and condition-indicator records. An image, a
minute-level feature row and a run are consequently different observational units.
Repeated views and long sensor sequences increase the number of stored observations
without increasing the number of independently acquired experiments to the same
extent.
          Experiment and run identifiers are retained through derived manifests. Tooth
identity and image role define target generation, whereas archive/member identity and
measurement provenance define sensor features. Their correspondence is principally at
run level. This supports joining measurements to a post-run proxy, rather than
asserting hardware-synchronized image/sensor events or prospective image availability.
Evidence covers these manifests and inspected implementation, with incomplete
independent reinspection of raw archive contents.



5.3        Dataset Challenges and Supervision Limitations

The target depends on acquisition geometry, illumination, texture and image-processing
choices as well as possible surface damage. In EXP-A and EXP-B, tooth indices 1–4
and 5–28 had different image-role coverage. Because tooth identity and view role were
confounded, comparing their candidate-area percentages could mix material differences
with acquisition differences. The v3 exclusion described below addresses this particular
problem; it does not independently validate the retained twenty-four teeth.
          The organizers reported possible overlap for EXP-A Run-2. A separate analysis
addressed equality between derived sequences. A tutorial reports equality of 372 feature
rows across 72 derived spectral features for two runs. That is evidence about its
extracted representation, not byte-level proof that complete raw archives are identical.
Both nominal runs remain visible in the historical analysis. The small number of
independent experiments, unequal run lengths and changing acquisition conditions limit
broad generalization even when train/test identifiers do not overlap.



                                            125
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


5.4     Preparation, Exploratory Analysis, and

        Pre-Checks

Preparation distinguished structural eligibility from scientific validity. Source manifests
retained experiment, run, tooth and image identities; sensor inspection considered
channel presence, units, finite values, temporal coverage and operating-state eligibility.
The early EXP-A exploration used runs 1, 3 and 5 and remains a deliberately narrower
historical analysis, not evidence that every later included run received the same
exploratory scrutiny. Its protected outputs were not rewritten to match the later all-run
population.
       Minute-level representations summarize process and sensor variables instead of
passing the entire waveform to the principal model. Timestamp and coverage checks
support sequence assembly, while aggregation can discard transients. The
high-frequency tutorial has different representation provenance. Duplicate diagnostics,
missingness masks and exclusions therefore remain tied to their producing
configurations; combining them would define a different dataset from that which
generated the historical predictions.



5.5     Task Formulation and Target-Version History

The current-end-of-run target is a percentage of a fixed visible-flank region classified as
candidate area. It is not remaining useful life, a native failure class, or a calibrated
physical spall-area measurement. A run-level target aggregates the largest three tooth
values. Raw target values and a causal monotonic transformation are separate
outcomes; monotonic processing cannot serve as independent evidence that the raw
measurements track deterioration.
       Version changes alter the quantity being predicted. Changing ROI geometry and
a residual threshold between v1 and v2 altered the proxy scale by approximately a
factor of six according to the target-version records. A median-view code state retained

Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction

the v2 label without a corresponding production artifact. Under v3, a canonical tooth
view is selected where available, otherwise the earliest camera-sequence view by
embedded time. Identical numerical constants in two versions can arise from unchanged
top-three teeth and do not prove identical measurement rules.



5.6       Investigation A: Image-Derived Target

          Construction and Assessment

The visual investigation has three distinct layers: producing a deterministic target,
fitting a model to that target, and assessing how closely that target represents physical
damage. The first two layers can be inspected from code, saved targets and prediction
artifacts. The third requires external reference evidence. Agreement with a heuristic
mask is insuﬀicient because it evaluates reproduction of the same rule that supplied
supervision. This distinction also applies to the detector trained on heuristic
pseudo-boxes: its localization metrics are defined relative to those boxes, not
expert-annotated defects. The separate scalar, detector and multitask routes therefore
remain different experiments with different outputs and comparison units.




                                              127
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


5.6.1     Motivation and Target Definition

Without organizer-supplied continuous damage labels, an explicit and reproducible
candidate-area statistic offered an engineering starting point. The implementation
measures dark, horizontally extended regions within a chosen flank ROI. Its percentage
denominator is the ROI area, so a change in ROI changes the numerical scale even if
the same pixels are selected. The term “candidate” is substantive: reflectance,
machining texture and acquisition artifacts may satisfy the image rule without being
physical damage. The target can consequently support a study of proxy prediction and
measurement sensitivity. It cannot by itself support a claim about tooth material loss
or maintenance necessity.


5.6.2     Image Processing and Aggregation

The inspected target code converts RGB to Lab luminance, applies CLAHE, estimates
a smooth background, and obtains a dark residual. Robust standardized residuals
define candidate pixels; morphological opening and closing and component-size/aspect
criteria retain extended regions. Candidate pixels divided by ROI pixels give the
per-view percentage. Historical v2 view maxima and the v3 deterministic-view rule then
produce tooth values, followed by the top-three run mean.
        The PHM scalar baseline invoked the Ultralytics RT-DETR-L prediction path
and recorded intermediate maps from three layers. The regression head used 768
spatially pooled image features and one additional value, extraction latency. Latency
depends on the computing environment and can confound interpretation of the
image-regression result. Separate PHM detection and multitask experiments used
heuristic pseudo-box supervision. The core REASSEMBLE classifier used the
ResNet18-vd CNN backbone inside its RT-DETR-R18vd checkpoint (Chapter 6).




                                          128
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


5.6.3     Assessment Procedure and Available Reference Evidence

Prediction error measures how well a model reproduces the target on held-out images,
teeth or runs under the specified split. The separate appearance assessment compares
selected images with human judgments. A continuous physical-area reference would
require independent measurements or area annotations. The retained appearance-based
and AnomalyDINO-style diagnostics are useful for finding conspicuous disagreements,
but their selection and development exposure prevent treating them as an untouched
validation cohort.
        The retained evidence contains no independent expert area masks or physical
reference measurements that establish population-wide target accuracy, calibrated area
percentages or a defensible damage/no-damage operating threshold. A prediction
cannot independently resolve ambiguity in the appearance used to construct its training
label. These limits constrain subsequent interpretation even if a target-emulation score
appears favorable.


5.6.4     Target Scaling and Validation Limits

The two reported baseline errors refer to different versions of the photographic target.
Under the fixed EXP-B-to-EXP-F comparison, the training-mean run MAE is 0.6797
percentage points for v2 and 0.7348 for v3. The difference follows target construction,
not scalar-versus-profile aggregation: the top-three mean of the mean sorted training
profile equals the corresponding scalar training mean by linearity.
        Detector diagnostics report very small attainable recall against dense heuristic
boxes and a mismatch between predicted-box and pseudo-box scales. Those
observations qualify this training recipe and supervision geometry. Activation energy
near an apparent surface region does not independently prove correct localization or
identify a unique causal failure mechanism. Likewise, the saved image-regression row
included in a later summary is explicitly not LOEO and lacks EXP-B predictions. Its
assessment population therefore differs from that of the twenty-run sensor LOEO
comparison. The retained visual work establishes reproducible implementation and

                                           129
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


consequential supervision limitations, rather than verified physical damage detection.



5.7      Investigation B: Sensor-Based Condition

         Prediction

The sensor investigation asks whether run-level inputs available without post-run
imaging predict the provisional condition statistic. Images supply supervision in this
task, not a deployable inference branch. The comparison includes a training-mean
constant, a single sensor encoder and combinations of sensor-feature groups.
Maintaining that distinction prevents the broad phrase “multimodal fusion” from
obscuring a change in experimental question. A successful result would establish
prediction of a particular proxy under a particular split. A failed result leaves several
possible explanations, including target noise, acquisition shift, limited independent
training support, representation loss and optimization limits. The observed comparison
alone does not identify which explanation dominates.


5.7.1     Inputs and Preprocessing

The retained minute-feature configuration names nine base variables: speed, torque,
temperature, axial RMS, radial RMS, FM4, NA4, M6A and ALR. Seven summaries per
base variable—mean, standard deviation, median, minimum, maximum, last value and
slope—plus missingness indicators yield 72 channels. These are variable-length
sequences of engineered features. They are not 72 raw accelerometer channels or a
raw-waveform Transformer input. A separate spectral analysis constructs a 72-feature
array from high-frequency vibration.
        The custom PHM model forms shared channel-wise patches and applies
positional encoding, a Transformer encoder and masked pooling. Padding and
any-valid-time patch masks are explicit in source. Their existence does not prove
historical invariance to batch length or resolve earlier patch-grid concerns. Train-only
transformations are supported for the principal LOEO runner; an audit-reported

                                            130
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


normalizer fitted before a different four-arm loop is a separate limitation.


5.7.2     Reference Predictors and Model Configurations

The training-mean constant is essential because the target scale and
between-experiment distribution can make a visually plausible prediction uncompetitive.
Ridge regression supplies a regularized non-temporal reference, with the retained
configuration searching alpha values 0.01, 0.1, 1, 10 and 100. The custom PatchTST
scalar path uses width 32, two encoder layers, four heads, feed-forward width 64, patch
length 16 and stride 8 in the inspected configuration. An optional profile head
constrains outputs to sorted ascending tooth statistics through positive increments; it
does not preserve positional tooth identity.
        The retained fused_all_three gives context, RMS and condition-indicator
groups separate encoders, concatenates their embeddings and applies a scalar head.
This is feature-level multi-source sensor fusion despite the class name
LateFusionRegressor; neither probability stacking nor visual–sensor inference
describes this path. The later four-arm code additionally distinguishes two RMS
sources and an empty encoder group, which is excluded. Its process-conditioned fusion
derives allocation from context features. These comparisons do not supply the
unavailable inference-time tooth image. Configured epoch caps remain separate from
actual fold histories.


5.7.3     Training and Evaluation Protocol

The historical fixed split trains on EXP-B, validates on EXP-A and assesses EXP-F. It
contains seven training, five validation and eight test runs. The principal retained
LOEO procedure instead rotates the assessment experiment while keeping a separate
remaining experiment for validation: train F/validate B/test A; train F/validate A/test
B; train B/validate A/test F. Thus “leave one experiment out” does not mean fitting on
both remaining experiments. Only three experiment identities support this analysis.
        The retained runner refits its normalizer on each training experiment. This is a

                                           131
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


materially stronger isolation claim than can be made for the distinct four-arm
diagnostic whose preprocessing was fitted outside its changing fold loop. The analysis
identified the target version from the selection settings, target hashes and source arrays
because some configuration labels referred to an earlier version. Missing historical fold
records limit retrospective verification.


5.7.4     Results and Uncertainty

Table 5.2 reports the retained twenty-run summary on the provisional target scale.
Lower MAE and RMSE are preferable; rank correlation addresses ordering rather than
calibration. The constant’s nonzero pooled rank statistic reflects changing fold-specific
constants, not informative within-fold ranking.

        The observed difference in mean absolute error between three-group fusion and
the training mean was +0.0196 percentage points across the twenty runs. The mean of
the paired run-bootstrap differences was +0.0177 percentage points, with a 95% interval
of [−0.1962, 0.2278]. The interval allows lower error for either predictor. The study used
no prespecified equivalence margin.


5.7.5     Interpretation and Limitations

The retained learned predictors do not establish an improvement over the constant
under this task and protocol. That conclusion is narrower than “the sensors have no
damage information.” The target may not align with the physical signal, a model can
be underfit or overfit, and cross-experiment acquisition differences may dominate the

                                               132
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


small training population. Twenty nominal runs also do not eliminate dependence
caused by repeated or equal derived sequences.
       Run-cluster intervals describe resampling of saved outcomes conditional on fitted
models. They do not quantify full training-seed variation or uncertainty over a
population of independent test machines. With only three experiments, the number of
genuinely different deployment domains remains small. The unresolved target meaning,
the profile’s loss of tooth identity and padding/provenance concerns additionally limit
interpretation. These qualifications do not erase a negative comparison; they identify
which hypothesis it actually challenges.



5.8     Additional Diagnostic Experiments and

        Evaluation Sensitivity

Supplementary analyses investigate why the original routes were insuﬀicient, without
replacing the principal comparison. The HF/spectral tutorial reports MAE 1.2203 for
its full 72-feature model against a v2 constant MAE 0.6797 on eight EXP-F runs.
Reduced-feature variants performed better than that full model but none beat its
constant. Repeatedly inspecting feature subsets on the same test split makes this a
development diagnostic, not independent confirmation of the selected subset. Its strong
causal language about overfitting or complete archive duplication exceeds what feature
tables alone establish and is not adopted here.
       The four-arm study compares group regressors, concatenation, aﬀine late fusion
and context-conditioned embeddings, but its preprocessing-isolation concern prevents
merging its results with principal LOEO. Raw-vibration and tooth-sector screens,
AnomalyDINO sweeps, odd/even views, synthetic controls, teacher/oracle constructions
and monotonic rescoring address separate uncertainties. An oracle or target-derived
synthetic feature can demonstrate pipeline capacity without demonstrating deployable
information. Detailed diagnostic tables belong in Appendix D with their own target and
exposure labels.


                                           133
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


5.9     What the PHM Study Establishes and Leaves

        Unresolved

Neither the single sensor encoder nor the three-group model improved on the training
mean in the observed run-level comparison. The paired interval for three-group fusion
included both lower and higher error. Changing the photographic target also changed
the constant predictor’s error on a fixed split. The comparison therefore challenges the
inference that adding sensor representations improves prediction of this proxy across
experiments.
       Optimizing a predictor for physical damage would first require checking the
proxy against independent damage measurements or expert area annotations. Until
then, the error estimates quantify agreement with the photographic rule. The study
also leaves advance warning, remaining useful life and response to natural sensor failure
unresolved. Differences in targets, prediction units and preprocessing, including the
four-arm experiment’s shared normalizer, prevent a direct comparison across all PHM
fusion models.



5.10      Implications for Subsequent Dataset and

          Architecture Choices

PHM was undertaken within the architecture programme motivated by robotic welding,
rather than as a prespecified feasibility stage designed around its eventual negative
result. The subsequent move to REASSEMBLE responded to supervision and
comparison limits: recorded action outcomes, visual and sensor inputs at a common
completed-action horizon, and more recording groups. The empirical task changed from
a photograph-derived gear-condition proxy to retrospective manipulation failure,
retaining the questions about representation, fusion and robustness. This progression
does not validate a welding classifier or establish that visual and vibration information
are generally uninformative.


                                           134
Chapter 5. PHM Case Study: Target Construction and Condition Prediction


      The design lesson is to establish the target, observation horizon and independent
evaluation units before optimizing fusion complexity. For PHM, a physical damage
measurement would establish what the proxy represents. The error of the constant
predictor provides a reference for interpreting model performance under degradation.
The subsequent study compares combined pipelines with their single-input reference
models and measures their responses to controlled corruption.




                                         135
Chapter 6

REASSEMBLE Case Study:
Representation, Fusion, and
Robustness

On 4,530 completed robotic actions, the logistic stacker ranked failures better than the
statistical sensor predictor (average precision 0.4473 versus 0.4294). Its advantage over
equal averaging (0.4457) remained unresolved, and none of the original
context-conditioned gates showed a consistent advantage. REASSEMBLE’s paired
video, machine signals and recorded outcomes made it possible to compare these
methods on the same actions. Later experiments examined alternative representations
and fusion rules on the same cohort.
       The core experiment compared fusion models on a common prediction bank.
The exploratory extensions and later visual studies used their own fitted models and
assessed each change separately.

Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness

(15.233 s). The saved failure label accompanies this example. Frame selection illustrates
temporal coverage. Source: Saved data-preparation example.




                                             137
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


6.1       Study Objectives and Research-Question

          Mapping

SQ1 concerns implemented visual and temporal representations relative to engineered
sensor statistics. SQ2 concerns early, late and adaptive fusion under shared recording
groups. SQ3 distinguishes known missing modalities, synthetic degradation of a present
modality and broader distribution shifts. SQ4 concerns the controls needed to interpret
these comparisons: native labels, group separation, inner selection, calibration, paired
uncertainty and computational budgets. The evaluation therefore includes simple
reference predictors and a strong unimodal competitor before interpreting multimodal
gains.
         The task is retrospective classification of an already completed action. It is
relevant to monitoring and automated inspection of industrial manipulation, but it is
not a direct replication of rotating-machine damage diagnosis. The observation horizon
includes the action’s end. Consequently, a representation that exploits late frames can
be useful here without demonstrating prediction before an unsuccessful event. The
dataset paper supplies acquisition context; the retained manifests and implementation
establish this thesis’s specific population and task (Sliwowski et al., 2025).



6.2       Dataset Overview and Native Outcome

          Annotations

The inventory contains 149 recordings; the retained common cohort contains 4,530
eligible actions from 148 recordings. A failure is one minus the native success indicator.
There are 509 failures, giving positive-class prevalence 0.1124. Failure frequency differs
substantially by action: pick has 98 failures in 1,190 actions; insert, 243 in 1,156;
remove, 146 in 1,096; and place, 22 in 1,088. This makes action identity a substantive
baseline. These filtered counts are distinct from the dataset paper’s broader acquisition
counts, because eligibility and task construction define the analysis population


                                             138
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


(Sliwowski et al., 2025).
       The native outcome describes task success, not a spatial defect mask, severity
score or verified physical root cause. One recording can contribute many actions, and
actions from a recording share acquisition context. Counting 4,530 actions as
independent experiments would overstate the amount of independent evidence.
Recording identities are accordingly used for outer splitting and uncertainty resampling.



6.3     Dataset Challenges and Modality Availability

The retained modalities are hand-camera images and machine-sensor measurements:
three force channels, three torque channels, two gripper channels, seven joint-effort
channels and seven joint-velocity channels. These twenty-two channels include
operating state as well as direct interaction measurements. Complementarity is thus an
empirical question, because both modalities may encode action identity or progress
rather than independent evidence of failure.
       Segment-offset and drift checks could not establish a common timing anchor for
audio, so the predictive experiment excluded the audio branch. This is an alignment
limitation, not a result showing that an audio model failed to learn. Similarly, seventeen
object categories do not establish seventeen disjoint physical objects. The available
relationship graph did not support a verified object-instance-disjoint test. The
recording-disjoint split evaluates transfer between recordings. Generalization to unseen
objects or sites and hardware-clock synchronization remain untested.



6.4     Preparation and Pre-Checks

Preparation established a common cohort before comparing architecture outputs.
Required information included native action boundaries and outcomes, valid camera
correspondence, suﬀicient sensor coverage, and a recording-group identity. These checks
support comparable inputs and prevent changing the evaluation population in response
to a particular model’s performance. They do not certify that every outcome label is

                                           139
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


unambiguous or that every sensor is physically calibrated.
        Eligibility checks and representation choices are distinct: temporal pooling
changes the view of an accepted action, whereas missing observations can affect
eligibility or require a missingness policy.


6.4.1     Cohort Construction and Exclusions

The core analysis uses exactly 4,530 common eligible actions and retains one outcome
per action. Inventory recordings and retained recordings are reported separately rather
than treating the unused recording as evidence of unexplained model failure. Actions
outside the four specified types or without usable required observations are outside this
cohort. Exclusion manifests, rather than model-score thresholds, define eligibility.
        Later studies sometimes assess only 753 of these actions. That population is the
original outer-fold-zero assessment subset, containing 77 failures in 26 recordings; it is
not a newly acquired independent holdout. Its prevalence, approximately 0.1023, differs
from the complete cohort’s prevalence. Because average precision depends on prevalence
and population composition, a 753-action AP cannot be ranked directly beside a pooled
4,530-action AP as if only architecture had changed.


6.4.2     Visual–Sensor Correspondence and Action Boundaries

Sixteen distinct frames are selected deterministically at approximately uniform
positions within each completed action. The selection uses the recorded action interval;
it does not learn where failure begins. Sensor observations are associated with that
same action and resampled to 512 normalized progress positions. These positions are
not a 512-Hz sampling rate. Normalized progress enables fixed-shape fitting across
different action durations while changing the temporal interpretation of features.
        Checks establish manifest-level correspondence and acceptable available coverage.
They do not prove sub-frame synchronization from a shared hardware clock. Boundary
errors can affect both modalities jointly, and late visual information may reflect the
already known outcome. The subsequent boundary review and temporal pooling

                                               140
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


experiment address parts of this problem, without converting the task into online
warning.


6.4.3      Missing, Invalid, and Inconsistent Observations

Sensor extraction requires at least 0.89 finite coverage per channel and avoids
extrapolation and interpolation across long gaps. Accepted residual missing values are
filled with training-partition means after channel standardization. Masks remain part of
the input preparation, but the retained PatchTST configuration does not convert the
observed mask into attention suppression. Passing a mask to an API and enforcing
missing-token attention are therefore different claims.
        Some proposed quality descriptors have limited information even on clean data.
The gripper median-absolute-deviation outlier statistic is undefined for 1,562 rows and
is imputed from training data; sixteen quality fields are constant. These details qualify
the interpretation of a quality-aware gate: its input name alone does not establish that
it receives a reliable independent quality estimate. Conversely, the deliberate
complete-modality-loss experiments supply a known availability mask, which is stronger
information than detecting an unnoticed hardware failure.


6.4.4      Human Annotation Review and Its Limits

One reviewer completed 64 selected clips. Of 59 determinate judgments, 57 agreed with
the native outcome and two disagreed; five of 64 were indeterminate. Agreement among
determinate cases is therefore 96.6%, while agreement over all reviewed cases is 89.1%.
These denominators answer different questions. Sampling balanced action and label
strata with recording spread rather than drawing an unstratified population sample, so
neither proportion is an unbiased dataset-wide error estimate.
        For action boundaries, sixty clips were acceptable, three questionable and one
indeterminate; four possible presentation artifacts were also flagged. Overlapping flags
identify ten unique clips requiring adjudication. A single reviewer completed the
assessment. Inter-rater agreement and adjudicated outcomes are therefore unavailable.

                                           141
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


Labels, folds and model metrics consequently retain their original definitions; the review
identifies specific uncertainties rather than supplying a replacement ground truth.


6.4.5     Group Definitions and Split Checks

Five outer folds hold out whole recordings, with four recording-disjoint inner folds for
selection. Outer assessment populations contain 753, 964, 1,037, 906 and 870 actions;
their failure counts are 77, 109, 125, 99 and 99, respectively. The corresponding
recording counts are 26, 31, 32, 29 and 30. Summing the assessment rows recovers all
4,530 actions, 509 failures and 148 recordings exactly.
        Within each outer training set, the visual and sensor branches supplied
out-of-fold (OOF) predictions for training the logistic stacker. Each branch predicted an
action using a fit that excluded its recording. During inner validation of the stacker,
however, every split reused this bank. Some rows used to train the stacker could
therefore come from branch fits that had seen recordings assigned to the stacker’s inner
validation split. This indirect dependency affects internal model and threshold
assessment. It does not extend to the outer test: those recordings were excluded from
branch fitting, calibration, stacker training and threshold selection. Fully independent
inner validation would require rebuilding the branch prediction bank within every
stacker-validation split, including repeated branch fits and calibration across all five
outer folds. This additional nesting was beyond the training budget, so the outer scores
evaluate the procedure that reused the outer-training bank.



6.5      Task Formulation, Model Inputs, and Shared

         Evaluation Protocol

The positive class is failure. The principal ranking measure is average precision (AP),
computed by the average-precision implementation rather than trapezoidal integration
of a precision–recall curve. AUROC, balanced accuracy, macro-F1, failure recall and
precision, Brier score and fifteen-bin equal-width expected calibration error (ECE)

                                            142
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


describe other aspects of performance. Precision–recall reporting is particularly
important at the observed minority-class prevalence (Saito & Rehmsmeier, 2015).
        Unless a later subsection states otherwise, reported core scores pool each action’s
outer-held-out probability across the five folds. Pooled AP is not the arithmetic mean
of five fold AP values. Training-only selection and sigmoid logistic calibration use
appropriate inner outputs. Hard-decision thresholds maximize inner balanced accuracy
over 0.01–0.99, with ties resolved toward 0.5. Calibration and threshold selection are
separate operations. Two thousand paired recording-cluster bootstrap resamples
quantify uncertainty conditional on the retained fitted models; they do not refit the
entire procedure or estimate all training-seed uncertainty. Nineteen structured
label-shift permutations preserve recording/action structure under their conditional
exchangeability assumption, giving a smallest plus-one probability of 0.05.



6.6       Experiment A: Unimodal Representations

The unimodal study establishes a reference hierarchy before fusion. Class-prior, majority
and action-type predictors reveal what can be achieved without detailed observations.
Engineered sensor statistics are then compared with the frozen visual representation
and a compact sensor Transformer. Identical retained action identifiers and recording
groups prevent changes in population from masquerading as representation gains.


6.6.1     Objective and Comparison Rationale

The unimodal comparison included simple predictors that can exploit process or sensor
information beyond class prevalence. The action-type predictor controls for the large
native failure-rate differences between pick, insert, remove and place. The
sensor-statistics model controls for useful low-complexity descriptions of force, torque
and motion. The two neural branches test different inductive biases within bounded
budgets; they do not represent exhaustive optimization of all visual or time-series
models.


                                            143
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


        Both neural branches improved AP over the action baseline. Their AUROC
differences remained unresolved. A later test of incremental predictive value motivated
the visual–sensor fusion comparison.


6.6.2     Visual Representation Configurations

The core visual extractor loads the COCO-pretrained RT-DETR-R18vd checkpoint
(PekingU/rtdetr_r18vd) at the recorded revision. From the loaded detector instance,
it selects wrapper.model.backbone.model, the checkpoint’s own ResNet18-vd CNN
backbone, and freezes its parameters. The forward pass reads this backbone’s feature
maps and bypasses the hybrid Transformer encoder, detection decoder, and box heads
(Y. Zhao et al., 2024).
        Frames are resized to 640 × 640 using bilinear interpolation, converted to RGB
and divided by 255 without ImageNet mean/standard-deviation normalization. Spatial
means from three scales concatenate to 896 features per frame; the sixteen frame
descriptors are averaged. A frozen extractor feeds a 896 → 128 → 1 ReLU/dropout
head with 114,945 trainable parameters. Whole-action averaging discards temporal
order. Weighted binary cross-entropy, AdamW with learning rate 0.001 and weight
decay 0.001, batch size 128 and gradient clipping at one are used. Inner selection
chooses five or ten epochs; outer budgets are 5, 5, 10, 10 and 5.


6.6.3     Statistical Sensor and PatchTST Configurations

The statistical representation computes ten features per channel: mean, standard
deviation, median, minimum, maximum, lower and upper quartiles, range, RMS and
progress slope. Twenty-two channels therefore produce 220 features. A
training-standardized, class-balanced L2 logistic model selects C from 0.1, 1 and 10 by
mean inner AP. A subsequent unweighted C = 1 logistic sigmoid calibrates selected
out-of-fold raw scores.
        The neural branch is a scratch-trained Hugging Face PatchTST-style model with
context 512 × 22, patch length 32, stride 16, hidden width 32, two layers, four heads

                                           144
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


and feed-forward width 64. Internal scaling is disabled; layer normalization and dropout
0.1 are used. Mean patch pooling and channel concatenation feed a 128-dimensional
projection and binary head, giving 109,505 trainable parameters. The observed mask is
passed but does not suppress attention in this configuration. Inner selection allows
three or six epochs and chooses six in every outer fold. This is a compact adaptation of
the patching idea, not a claim to reproduce all settings of the original PatchTST paper
(Nie et al., 2023).


6.6.4     Results and Computational Considerations

Table 6.1 reports pooled assessment scores. The majority predictor has AP equal to
prevalence, while the action predictor improves ranking substantially. Sensor statistics
are the strongest retained unimodal representation. Relative to statistics, PatchTST
changes AP by −0.1878 with a paired 95% interval [−0.2314, −0.1407] and AUROC by
−0.0740 [−0.1004, −0.0469]. Similar trainable head sizes therefore do not imply similar
predictive utility.

Sensor statistics                .4294            .7793          .6149         .0804        .0174
Frozen visual mean               .2307            .6978          .6621         .0943        .0070
Short-budget PatchTST            .2416            .7054          .5639         .0937        .0067

        The AP intervals are [.3801, .4828] for statistics, [.1983, .2701] for vision and
[.2102, .2826] for PatchTST. The visual pipeline also includes video decoding and frozen
CNN extraction, unlike fitting directly on compact statistics. In the retained
warm-component benchmark, statistical sensors took 26.267 ms, vision 478.415 ms, F2
504.693 ms and F6 564.021 ms. These measurements used a T4, four CPU threads,
three deterministic segments, sensor arrays in memory and cached compressed video;
fusion times are sequential component sums. They establish a bounded practical cost

                                               145
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


difference rather than cold-start throughput or a real-time deployment guarantee.
Counting only F2’s three learned combination parameters would omit the branch heads
and frozen visual backbone.


6.6.5     Interpretation and Implications for Fusion

Visual AP exceeds the action reference by 0.0499 [.0225, .0836], and PatchTST exceeds
it by 0.0608 [.0293, .0974]. Their AUROC differences, 0.0127 [−.0154, .0406] and 0.0203
[−.0115, .0494], remain unresolved. These are metric-specific findings, not equivalence
results. Lower neural AP than sensor statistics also does not exclude incremental
information: useful errors can differ even when one standalone predictor is weaker.
        The fusion experiment therefore uses the statistical sensor predictor as its
reference. Each fitted fusion pipeline is compared with that predictor on the same
held-out actions. The stacker also fits a new logistic map to the branch probabilities.
The saved visual term’s contribution to within-fold ordering is examined below.
Attribution of the complete pooled gain remains limited by the absent matched
sensor-only second-stage control. A separate follow-up tests how the training budget
affects PatchTST performance.



6.7      Experiment B: Fusion Strategies

Six fusion families share the cohort and recording structure: probability averaging,
stacking, representation concatenation and three context-dependent gates. Their input
spaces and calibration stages differ.


6.7.1     Objective and Hypotheses

The first hypothesis is that the combined visual–sensor pipeline improves held-out
failure ranking relative to the retained sensor pipeline. The second is that action or
quality context improves allocation between branches. These questions are distinct: a
fixed map can use complementary predictions without an effective per-instance


                                            146
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


reliability estimate. The comparison changes both inputs and the fitted probability
map. Within-fold ordering can nevertheless identify a visual contribution beyond a
strictly increasing transformation of the sensor score. A matched sensor-only
second-stage model, which was not completed, would strengthen attribution of the full
pipeline contrast. Both sensor-only and simple static fusion references are therefore
needed when interpreting adaptive models.
        Clean gains do not establish corruption recognition; the subsequent experiment
tests frozen clean-trained models directly.


6.7.2      Compared Architectures and Exact Fusion Formulations

F1 averages calibrated probabilities: p = (pv + ps )/2. F2 fits a fixed-C = 1 L2 logistic
stacker,
                                  p = σ(b + av pv + as ps ).                             (6.1)

It has three scalar parameters. Its signed coeﬀicients are unconstrained and do not form
convex reliability weights. F3 instead projects the 128-dimensional visual representation
and 220 sensor statistics to separate 128-dimensional vectors, concatenates and
layer-normalizes them, and uses a hidden layer of width 32 or 64. Candidate training
budgets are ten or twenty epochs.
        F4–F6 use a masked softmax gate and calibrated branch logits:
                                                               (                   )
                                                                   ∑
           w = softmax(h(u) + availability mask),       p=σ            wm logit(pm ) .   (6.2)
                                                                   m


F4 receives four action indicators and two availability indicators. F5 receives thirty-six
quality descriptors and availability; F6 receives all forty-two inputs. Hidden widths are
eight or sixteen. The gates have no final intercept correction or post-gate calibrator.
Context or quality can encode process content as well as reliability; interpreting a
learned weight as causal sensor trust is therefore unsupported.




                                              147
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


6.7.3     Training, Calibration, and Evaluation

Fusion uses branch prediction banks with recording-excluded row construction and
held-out outer assessment recordings. The internal bank-reuse dependency described
above remains part of its selection procedure. F2 stacks calibrated probabilities,
whereas F4–F6 mix calibrated logits. F2 fixes C = 1 and has no further calibration
stage. F3 uses weighted binary cross-entropy and a sigmoid calibrator; the gates use
unweighted binary cross-entropy, forty epochs, learning rate 0.003 and weight decay
0.001. These differences in link, objective and calibration opportunity are part of the
recipes, rather than controls that isolate adaptation alone.
        Gate results average probabilities from seeds 20260927–20260929. Averaging
probabilities is not equivalent to inserting averaged weights into a sigmoid. The core
threshold protocol and paired recording bootstrap remain in force. These controls
improve comparability but do not equalize every architecture’s effective capacity,
objective or calibration opportunity.


6.7.4     Predictive Results and Uncertainty

F2 has AP 0.4473 and AUROC 0.7950. The paired differences for F2 minus the
statistical sensor predictor were 0.0179 [.0086, .0283] in AP and 0.0156 [.0060, .0255] in
AUROC. The Brier score difference was −0.0020 (95% CI [−.0031, −.0009]), indicating
lower probability error for F2. The ECE difference −0.0036 [−.0127, .0099] and recall
difference −0.0039 [−.0255, .0163] remain unresolved. The more precise AP contrast is
+0.017907 [.008603, .028284]. Against probability averaging, F2’s AP gain is only
+0.001556 [−.007607, .011165], which does not establish a discrimination advantage.
Thus the retained evidence supports a modest combined-pipeline increment over
sensors, with improved probability quality on some metrics, rather than a general
superiority of learned stacking over simple averaging.
        All three original gates have lower AUROC than F2, with intervals excluding
zero for those differences. Their AP differences remain unresolved. The AP difference
for F6 minus F2 was −0.0059 [−.0248, .0137]. The failure-recall difference for F6 minus

                                           148
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness

F2 was −0.0393 (95% CI [−.0649, −.0122]).


6.7.5     Modality Complementarity and Error Analysis

At the frozen hard-decision thresholds, both unimodal branches are correct on 2,347
actions and both wrong on 529. Vision alone is correct on 486, while sensors alone are
correct on 1,168. Among 509 failures, 240 are identified by both, 99 by neither, 97 by
vision alone and 73 by sensors alone. Failures detected by vision alone occurred in 61
recordings.
        An oracle that always selected a correct branch would identify 410 of 509
failures, approximately 0.8055 recall. That is an unavailable truth-dependent upper
reference, not an achieved fusion result or a deployable gate. Complementarity provides
potential information, while learning which branch to trust from available inputs
remains a separate problem. These counts describe decisions at fixed thresholds. Paired
AP comparisons assess the ranking of the same actions across thresholds.


6.7.6     Interpretation and Comparison Limits

Independent saved-prediction recomputation separates within-fold ordering from
cross-fold score alignment. All five F2 sensor coeﬀicients are positive. Omitting the
visual term while retaining the jointly fitted parameters, qs,k = σ(bk + αs,k ps ), therefore
preserves sensor ordering within fold k. Its AP and AUROC match the sensor branch
exactly within every fold. Restoring vision improves AUROC in all five folds and AP in
four; the remaining AP difference is −0.00469. Thus, the visual term supplies beneficial



                                             149
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


observed ordering changes beyond a strictly increasing transformation of the same
sensor probabilities, although its AP benefit is not uniform. Appendix D gives the
fold-level check.
        Pooled AP is .42939 for sensors, .43092 for the reduced score and .44730 for
complete F2. Different fold-specific maps can change pooled ordering while preserving
every within-fold ranking. This omission is an arithmetic diagnostic, not a refitted
sensor-only control or a missing-modality experiment. It does not uniquely allocate the
pooled gain to vision, establish information conditional on all sensor features, or
quantify causal modality importance. AP changes also do not measure calibration. The
folds share training populations; historical recording-bootstrap intervals condition on
fixed models and omit training and repeated selection uncertainty. These findings do
not establish population-wide superiority or a resolved advantage over equal averaging.
        Within the tested representations and budgets, neither intermediate fusion nor
the original adaptive gates establishes an advantage over the successful static reference.
This finding is architecture- and protocol-specific. The later anchored model changes
the link and learns a different function class; its positive exploratory AP result is
therefore compatible with the original gates’ bounded negative evidence.



6.8      Experiment C: Missing and Degraded

         Modalities

The core robustness study froze the clean-trained statistical sensor and visual predictors
(U1 and U2), equal averaging (F1), the stacker (F2), and the two gates using quality
descriptors (F5 and F6). It perturbed one input at a time and also tested known loss of
a full input stream.


6.8.1     Robustness Questions and Tested Conditions

The central question is whether a gate both changes allocation in the expected direction
and limits predictive deterioration relative to F2. Absolute performance at high severity

                                            150
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


is also necessary: a weak clean model can show a small decline merely because little
useful ranking remains to lose. An intact unimodal branch supplies a practical reference
for whether fusion introduces avoidable damage.
        Clean decision thresholds remain frozen during corruption. This matches a
deployment in which the operator does not receive fault labels and recalibrate at every
severity. Known availability masks in complete-dropout tests are an explicitly different
information condition. The two sets of results measure responses to different
information about the fault.


6.8.2     Corruption Operators and Severity Definitions

Visual operators include pixel Gaussian noise with standard deviations 0.02, 0.05 and
0.10 on the normalized scale, Gaussian blur at severities one, two and four, and removal
of two, five or eight of sixteen selected frames. Sensor operators include Gaussian noise
scaled by 0.1, 0.5 or 1.0 training-channel standard deviations; removal of two, seven or
eleven channels; missing temporal blocks covering 10%, 30% or 50% of the sequence;
and six force/torque channels stuck at training medians.
        Sensor statistics and quality descriptors are recomputed from the altered input,
rather than retaining clean summaries beside corrupted signals. The core uses its fixed
operator realization protocol. A later augmentation study averages across five
stochastic test realizations. Its Gaussian-noise estimates therefore refer to a different
evaluation protocol. None of these operators is direct evidence of cross-machine shift,
clock drift or physical sensor recalibration.


6.8.3     Missingness Policies and Dropout Experiments

In the original complete-dropout test, the four evaluated fusion models used the
statistical sensor probability and threshold when vision was absent. When sensors were
absent, they used the visual predictor and its threshold. The evaluator received the
identity of the missing stream. The core gate rejects a sample with both streams absent.
The robustness report contains no empirical result for that condition. A later

                                            151
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


quality-interaction pilot marked such predictions unavailable.
        A separate F6 training experiment samples visual-only dropout with probability
0.15, sensor-only dropout with probability 0.15 and both-present inputs with
probability 0.70. Both-absent inputs are never sampled. Because a
one-available-modality softmax has weight exactly one and the branches are fixed, its
missing-case output is structurally fixed and supplies no useful gradient for improving
the fallback prediction. Dropout training cannot make that output better under this
architecture. Its clean AP is 0.4377, changing ordinary F6 by −0.0037 [−.0085, .0003].


6.8.4     Predictive Performance and Calibration Under

          Degradation

Under strongest pixel Gaussian noise, F2 retains AP 0.3963, while F5 and F6 fall to
0.1288 and 0.1275. The intact sensor baseline remains at AP 0.4294. F2’s Brier score
nevertheless rises to 0.2968 and ECE to 0.4368, compared with clean sensor values
0.0804 and 0.0174. Ranking retention therefore conceals severe probability failure.
        Under sensor Gaussian noise at one training standard deviation, F2 AP is 0.2222
and F5/F6 are 0.1753/0.1788, below intact vision at 0.2307. F2’s Brier/ECE rise to
0.4798/0.5251. Strong blur gives F2/F5/F6 AP 0.4099/0.4061/0.4179, without a
resolved general gate advantage. These conditions demonstrate that combining an
intact and corrupted modality can underperform the intact branch. Detection of
corruption and calibrated uncertainty remain unresolved rather than being supplied
automatically by a fusion layer.


6.8.5     Gate Behaviour and Fallback Behaviour

No tested original gate/family combination establishes both an appropriate allocation
response and a smaller decline under the study’s criteria; no supported absolute
high-severity AP superiority follows. A gate weight can change with a quality input
without changing in a beneficial way. Conversely, a smaller performance decline from a
weaker clean starting point is insuﬀicient to establish improved reliability.

                                            152
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


        Exact missing-modality equality follows programmed fallback. It demonstrates
consistent handling of known absence, while corruption recognition and useful recovery
remain separate capabilities. This is narrower than training an encoder or student to
retain performance after input loss, as in transferable missing-modality methods (Choi
& Lee, 2019; M. Ma et al., 2022; Neverova et al., 2015).


6.8.6     Interpretation and Untested Conditions

The core evidence rejects a broad claim of reliable adaptation for the tested gates. The
AP, calibration and intact-branch comparisons capture different consequences of
corruption. Robustness to known complete absence is largely architectural here;
robustness to unnoticed corruption is empirically weak.
        Natural missingness mechanisms, simultaneous partial failures, asynchronous
clocks, new machines, new operating sites and prospective early warning were not
established. The later augmentation study changes training exposure and tests several
held-out operator families, but the same recording population and already observed core
faults limit claims of independent discovery. An untested condition remains open rather
than receiving a favorable interpretation from the model’s name.



6.9      Follow-up Investigations

Three exploratory comparisons on the same cohort yielded higher average precision
under their respective protocols. Last-quarter visual pooling with static stacking
reached 0.4934, anchored adaptation reached 0.4683, and combining long-budget
PatchTST with sensor statistics reached 0.4403. Each had its own control. The
temporal-pooling study was completed separately from an earlier visual experiment that
stopped after partial execution. Other follow-ups examined matched corruption
exposure and visual tuning.




                                          153
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


6.9.1     Longer Sensor Training and Alternative Sensor

          Representations

The longer-budget experiment retains the PatchTST inputs and architecture, permits
up to one hundred epochs, and uses minimum ten epochs with patience twelve. No
long-budget inner fit reaches the cap; outer budgets use rounded median inner best
epochs. Against a matched short-budget control, AP rises from 0.2557 to 0.2853, a
difference of 0.0297 [.0122, .0451]. The original short-budget experiment yielded 0.2416
AP under its own protocol.
        Long training remains below statistics: AP difference −0.1441 [−.1861, −.1009].
A fixed-C = 1 stacker combining long PatchTST and statistics reaches AP 0.4403,
improving statistics by 0.0110 [.0054, .0181]. This combines two representations of the
same sensor modality. It qualifies the original budget-dependent neural result while
preserving the strong engineered baseline.


6.9.2     Alternative Visual Pooling

The boundary-aware experiment exactly reproduces the original whole-segment mean
before changing pooling. AP is 0.2556 for two halves, 0.3080 for thirds, 0.3191 for the
last quarter, 0.2922 for early-plus-late and 0.2250 for an ordered GRU. The original
mean is 0.2307. Last-quarter minus mean AP is 0.0885 [.0424, .1293]; the protocol’s
simultaneous lower bound across five AP contrasts remains positive at 0.0301.
        This supports selected temporal aggregation changes, not a general
recurrent-model advantage or an isolated causal effect of temporal order. The GRU
changes capacity and inductive bias, while bin concatenation changes classifier inputs.
The selected last-quarter rule exploits complete-action information. Its separate F2
follow-up reaches AP 0.4934, with the more precise value 0.493437 versus original F2
0.447299: difference +0.0461 [.0260, .0632], and AUROC difference +0.0403
[.0291, .0522]. This is a meaningful exploratory same-cohort result after representation
selection, not independent confirmation or warning before the outcome. It was not



                                             154
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


combined with the anchored adaptive gate or the same-sensor hybrid.


6.9.3     Spatial-Encoder and Backbone Comparisons

A separate five-fold comparison tests CNN-only against CNN plus the spatial hybrid
encoder, with the same sixteen frames, 4,530 actions, frozen extraction and five-epoch
heads. It uses its own fitting and calibration subsets. CNN-only visual AP is 0.221644
and F2 AP 0.440764; hybrid-encoder visual AP is 0.171915 and F2 AP 0.424979, with
shared sensor AP 0.420926. The hybrid-minus-CNN F2 difference is −0.015785
[−0.024804, −0.005800]. Executing more of the detector therefore worsens this
particular comparison, rather than automatically improving representation quality.
        The descriptors also differ in width: CNN multiscale pooling yields 896 features
and the spatial hybrid-encoder path 768. Head size, active components and compute
change with that representation. The result cannot isolate an attention effect, and the
attention is spatial rather than across video time. Neither path executes the
object-detection decoder. CNN-only extraction, spatial-encoder extraction, final-stage
tuning and complete detector training remain distinct interventions.


6.9.4     Frozen Versus Fine-Tuned Visual Representations

A matched two-epoch diagnostic uses thirty-two frames for both frozen and
final-stage-tuned arms across the full 4,530-action cohort. It covers two seeds, with
seventy-five newly executed and twenty-five reused fits. Epoch matching does not imply
compute matching. Its fixed sensor recipe has pooled AP 0.4208, distinct from the core
selected-C baseline’s 0.4294.
        For seed 42, visual AP changes from 0.2227 to 0.2306, but the difference 0.0079
has interval [−.0173, .0332]; F2 changes from 0.4394 to 0.4386. For seed 20261023,
visual AP changes from 0.2168 to 0.2005, and F2 from 0.4352 to 0.4324. The
corresponding paired intervals include zero. These results do not identify a unique cause
for the absence of a fusion gain, such as calibration or out-of-fold mismatch; two seeds
also do not fully characterize seed variability.

                                            155
Chapter 6. REASSEMBLE Case Study: Representation, Fusion, and Robustness


        The later twenty-epoch visual cap completes twenty inner and five outer fits for
seed 42. Pooled visual AP is 0.3121, while mean fold AP is 0.3086. The additional fifty
fits for two more seeds are deferred; their absence is not a failure of the twenty-five
completed fits. Twenty-epoch F2 was not executed because its controller expected the
larger bank. A current checkpoint validator’s twenty-five-source-epoch requirement
conflicts with the twenty-epoch donor; that is an eligibility/configuration conflict, not
evidence of failed training.
        The hundred-epoch pilot restarts both arms from pretrained weights with fresh
heads, rather than continuing the twenty-epoch run. Both arms execute one hundred
epochs and select epochs fourteen (frozen) and forty-seven (full). On the same
753-action assessment subset, visual AP is 0.1938/0.2126 and F2 AP 0.3350/0.3491.
Full-minus-frozen F2 AP is 0.0141 [−.0008, .0291], leaving the difference unresolved.
Restricted historical F2 is 0.3672 on these same rows; its full-cohort AP is not the
relevant comparator.


6.9.5     Adaptive-Fusion Extensions and Matched Controls

Anchored adaptation preserves the successful F2 link and learns bounded adjustments:
                               (                                   )
